**Legacy folder:** `docs/` (older files — migrate here when you touch that feature)

---

## How to use this index

1. **Find a topic** in the catalog below (by ID, title, or area).
2. **Open the file** linked in the **File** column.
3. When you **add or change** a feature, update its doc and add a row to **Index revision log** at the bottom.
4. Copy [`_DOC_TEMPLATE.md`](./_DOC_TEMPLATE.md) for new topics.

| DOC-077 | Missing Receipt Files — OPEN INCIDENT | [`missing-receipt-files-incident.md`](./missing-receipt-files-incident.md) | 2026-08-01 | in progress | **Open data-loss incident, not a fix.** SELECT-only audit found ~1,875 `attachments` rows with no storage object: ~1,865 on/before 2026-05-27 from a partial storage restore (~2026-05-30) where DB rows were restored but objects were not, plus 10 after. Active defect identified: `finalize-expense-receipts` copies `temp/{userId}/{file}` → `{expenseId}/{file}` and never deletes the source (Q7 confirms temp twins persist), but `FileUpload.tsx:255` uploads **directly** to `{expenseId}/` when the expense already exists — so receipts added while editing have exactly one copy and no safety net. | DOC-079 | Review Expenses V2 Architecture | [`review-expenses-v2-architecture.md`](./review-expenses-v2-architecture.md) | 2026-08-01 | in progress | Audit + design for replacing the V1 Review Expenses loading pipeline. V1 accumulated 11 overlapping mechanisms (keyset + legacy offset pagination, missing-RPC detection, capability cache, Load All, localStorage caches, four client-side enrichment passes) producing 1,000-row truncation, ~1,512 of ~6,088 rows, blank states, 57014 timeouts and request storms. V2 separates list / detail / actions / filter-metadata into versioned RPCs returning a single `jsonb` document (bypasses the PostgREST row cap), with page-first CTE ordering so only page rows are enriched, snapshot+offset pagination with deterministic id tie-breakers, server-side search/filter/custom-field predicates, and a default-off `VITE_REVIEW_QUEUE_V2_ENABLED` flag with no silent V2→V1 fallback. **Rev 2.0 — SQL contracts + client data layer implemented (flag default OFF, migration not applied):** the audit found three required response fields that are not columns (`manager_queue_ready` is client-computed, `submitted_at` does not exist, `audit_logs.organization_id` is contradictory between generated types and an index migration). | DOC-080 | Review Expenses — Server Queue, Manual Employee Search & Debug Surface | [`debug-review-expenses-manual-employee-search.md`](./debug-review-expenses-manual-employee-search.md) | 2026-08-11 | deployed | **Rev 9.0 — admin bulk-approval checkbox fixed live on Production.** Owner tested Production Manager Review as the MicroLink admin: no row checkboxes, every submitted row "View", Actionable 1 of 9157. Read-only SSH audit proved the queue helper `_debug_review_actionability_v1` marked every pending row `not_assigned_to_caller`/`view_only` for the admin because it never implemented the org-admin super-approver rule that `review_approve_expense` V1/V2 and `ExpenseService` enforce — the queue disagreed with the approval RPC. Migration `20260810120000` adds the admin fast-path (approve/reject/correct on admin-approvable non-terminal statuses, any tier), applied live to `supabase-db-evo3o0…` with backup + SQL contract test + before/after re-audit (9850 pending now `admin_approvable`). Frontend unchanged (server-driven). Replaces the V1 Review Expenses loading pipeline with a proven server-side queue, keeping the existing UI. Live diagnostic proved a search collision: employee `6174` owns **24** expenses, but only **1** row matched the query — transaction `06174`, owned by a different employee (6557). Identity enrichment was 504-ing under bulk `expense_form_field_values` loads (37,593 rows), so `employee_id` never resolved client-side and all 24 rows silently vanished. **Rev 3.0 — runtime proven and wired into `/review` behind a default-off flag:** capabilities report `ready:true` at `schema_revision 20260801150000`, and the debug page returned **24/24 rows with 24/24 identities resolved** in 1,941 ms with transaction 06174 absent. The proven data layer is extracted into `src/features/reviewQueueServer/` (schemas, RPC arguments, employee-search state, cursor handling, identity parsing, filter serialization, error classification) with the debug feature a thin alias over it and scoped caches, so there is exactly one implementation. `/review` renders a wrapper that picks EngineerReview (default, untouched) or the server-backed page from `VITE_REVIEW_EXPENSES_SERVER_QUEUE_ENABLED`, with **no automatic fallback**. One search bar preserves the employee-vs-transaction separation (`txn:`/`#` bypasses employee lookup); typing issues zero requests. KPI cards read `scope_summary`; pagination is keyset with only walked page numbers; custom-field filters key on stable template/branch UUIDs. Additive `20260801150000` also fixed role/tier-blind actionability, row-duplicating identity joins and NULL-evaluating keyset predicates. 249 feature tests, 2046 total (was 1822); i18n 184 and lint 458 unchanged baselines. **Actions flag remains false; the production page has not been opened against a live database and visual parity is not human-verified.** **Rev 5.0 — actions connected and the detail modal made visually identical to the All Expenses dialog:** `src/features/reviewActions/` wraps the existing `ExpenseService` verify/approve/reject/correct methods rather than adding a mutation path (`assertActionsEnabled()` first in every path, no optimistic updates, `BULK_CONCURRENCY = 3`, batch rows excluded, client-side eligibility that can only subtract). The modal's invented presentation (`max-w-3xl`, nested cards, compact field grids, plain `<a>` attachment links, green-outline Approve) is replaced with the reference's own widths, typography, attachment rows, thumbnails and Cancel/Correct/Reject/Verify/Approve footer order and variants, reusing `ExpenseBaseFieldsDisplay`, `ExpenseFormFieldsDisplay`, `ExpenseReviewTimeline`, `ExpenseCorrectionAuditList` and `AttachmentPreviewDialog` fed from the single detail RPC — zero extra requests. Three presentational components extracted to `src/components/expense-review/`. One deliberate divergence: header and footer sit outside the scroll container, so the actions no longer scroll away on a long expense. 29 new tests, 2168 total; lint 458 unchanged. **Correct expense is withheld until the correction editor is built; actions flag still default-off; no SQL executed.** **Rev 6.0 — the main Review Expenses UI restored over the server queue.** Rev 5.0 matched AdminPanel's All Expenses dialog rather than the Review Expenses one, and never touched the table: every row rendered one generic `View / Review` button and `onView={setDetailId}` opened the singular modal for batch lines too, inviting a reviewer to approve one line of a batch and abandon the rest. `src/components/expense-review/*` was deleted rather than extended. The table is now main's — `useResizableColumns` widths under main's storage key, blue mono transaction number, violet Batch badge, green/red balance with an insufficient-balance warning, status badge plus queue hint, Resubmitted override, sticky Status/Actions. Batch rows render `Review batch` and navigate straight to `/expenses/batch/:id/review?from=review&expense=:id` with the queue state in router state, never setting `detailId` and never issuing the detail RPC; singular rows show `View/Approve` or `View` per the server's authority. The modal is main's card hierarchy and footer, and **Correct expense is fully restored** using main's own custom-field loading, validation, branch pruning and patch builder. Mobile matches `MobileReviewExpenseCard`. Documented differences: the batch button keeps the pending blue because batch completion cannot be computed honestly from one page; actionability is read from the server; no line-items card. 46 new tests, 2185 total; lint 458 unchanged; no SQL executed. **Rev 7.0 — employee filter, filtered KPIs and batch colours.** Selecting employee 6174 showed an empty table because `onResolved()` carried no information (so a leftover Level 2 Verified filter intersected the employee) and because `useCursorHistory` reset in an effect, leaving the previous query's cursor on the render that first carried the new employee — the RPC was asked for rows after position ~4,000 of the unfiltered ordering and returned none, which was then cached. Search now emits a typed `SearchResolution`; the cursor resets during render; submits are serialised. Additive read-only migration `20260802030000` adds `filtered_summary` (aggregated over the filtered scope before the cursor and page limit) and authoritative `batch_action_state` across every assigned line of each visible batch in one GROUP BY. Cards switch source by context and treat a missing `filtered_summary` as a contract mismatch, never zero. Batch buttons blue/amber/grey, grey meaning no remaining action for this reviewer and still clickable. 34 new tests, 2219 total; lint 458 unchanged. |

| DOC-085 | HEIC / HEIF receipt preview | [`heic-attachment-preview.md`](./heic-attachment-preview.md) | 2026-08-05 | in progress | HEIC is the iPhone camera default and no Chromium or Firefox build can decode it in an `<img>`. Upload-time conversion has existed since `uploadCompression.ts`, but pre-conversion receipts and uploads whose conversion failed (that branch deliberately stores the ORIGINAL) still reach the preview as raw HEIC — and `AttachmentPreviewDialog` had a single `previewError` flag that both "storage returned nothing" and "the `<img>` refused to decode" set, so an intact HEIC receipt was reported as *"This file is no longer available in storage… not recovered during migration"*. The app was claiming data loss that had not occurred. Now the resolver decodes HEIC→JPEG at read time via the existing `heic2any` dependency (dynamic import, ~341 kB gzip lazy chunk, loaded only on first HEIC preview), detecting by content type **or** extension **or** an ISO-BMFF `ftyp` brand sniff — the last mattering because storage routinely serves these as `application/octet-stream`. `resolveImagePreviewSource` returns `ok` / `missing` / `unsupported` plus both a renderable URL and the untouched original, so Download and Open in new tab always hand back the real uploaded file while the `<img>` shows the converted one. **Open in new tab is no longer PDF-only** — it renders for any resolved attachment and again inside the failure block. 18 new tests, 2277 total; tsc, eslint and build clean; no migration, no data change. **Not verified in a browser against a real HEIC receipt — decode is exercised against a mocked `heic2any` only.** **Rev 2.0 — hotfix: rev 1.0 hung the preview on "Loading image…" in production.** heic2any decodes in a blob Worker whose libheif glue calls `new Function`; the enforced CSP carries no `unsafe-eval`, so the worker threw EvalError and died and heic2any's promise never settled — an unbounded await no try/catch could rescue. Bounded the decode (20 s, always terminal), probe `new Function` and skip the decoder plus its 1.35 MB chunk when eval is barred, and try `createImageBitmap` first so Safari/iOS render the original with no conversion. Chromium/Firefox now fail fast with the format message and Open in new tab instead of hanging; **they still do not render HEIC** — that needs either `unsafe-eval` app-wide or server-side decode, an open decision recorded in the doc. 2280 tests. |
| DOC-086 | HEIC receipt preview — full incident & work report | [`heic-receipt-preview-incident-report.md`](./heic-receipt-preview-incident-report.md) | 2026-08-06 | in progress | Chronological narrative of the whole HEIC investigation, companion to DOC-085. Covers all four defects and the order they became visible: (1) one `previewError` flag shared by "storage returned nothing" and "the browser cannot decode these bytes", so intact iPhone receipts were reported as lost during migration; (2) the fix then hung forever because `heic2any` decodes in a blob Worker whose libheif glue calls `new Function`, the production CSP has no `unsafe-eval`, the worker died and its promise never settled — a try/catch cannot rescue a promise that never completes; (3) restoration outran the capabilities query; (4) the deployed edge function returned 500 on every conversion. Records why server-side decode was chosen over weakening CSP app-wide, the Coolify deployment mechanics (5 Supabase stacks on one host, `docker cp` because the SSH account cannot write `/data/coolify` and the repo's own scp-based deploy script therefore fails, credential-free verification via the 401 JSON), and the 500 diagnosis: the pixel buffer reached through a wasm view to the ENTIRE heap, but the operative cause was the isolate being OOM-killed at the default 150 MB per-worker limit. **Open: whether 150 MB suffices — raising it affects every function on that stack and is an owner decision.** |

Fixed by `20260808130000_get_backend_health_rpc.sql`: SECURITY DEFINER, STABLE, pinned `search_path`, reachability probed with `PERFORM … LIMIT 1` so no user record or object key can reach the caller, each probe in its own EXCEPTION block so one dead subsystem downgrades to `degraded` instead of failing the call, REVOKE PUBLIC/anon + GRANT authenticated only. Refresh-token 400 already self-heals via `recoverFromInvalidAuthSession`; WebSocket warning is sign-out/sign-in lifecycle. 15 new tests, incl. a guard that the hook's RPC name and the migration cannot drift apart again. Migration APPLIED and verified live 2026-08-08 — `SELECT public.get_backend_health()` returns `healthy` with all three reachability flags `true`; grants confirmed. Code on `dev` only, not merged. |
| DOC-089 | Approved expense — atomic balance deduction | [`approved-expense-atomic-balance-deduction.md`](./approved-expense-atomic-balance-deduction.md) | 2026-08-08 | in progress | Confirmed Production incident: an approved expense did not reduce the employee's balance. Approval was server-side but **deduction was client-side** — the browser computed `currentBalance - amount` and wrote `profiles.balance` filtered only by `user_id`. Engineers have no UPDATE policy on `profiles`, so for every engineer approval that write was RLS-filtered to **zero rows**; PostgREST does not error on a zero-row UPDATE, so the client's revert branch never ran and the audit logged what JavaScript had calculated rather than what Postgres committed. MicroLink evidence (anonymised): `standard` strategy — so **not** strategy-specific — 404 active profiles, 6 approved expenses, ₹880 total, 3 employees, all 6 approved by engineers. Repo V1 verified to match live Production exactly (locks expense, approves, never touches balance) — no drift. Fix is additive: new `expense_balance_deductions` table (UNIQUE per expense, RLS with no client write grant) and `review_approve_expense_v2`, which locks the expense then the single `organization_id + user_id` profile (never `LIMIT 1`), **wraps V1** so authorization keeps one implementation, does the arithmetic in SQL with `RETURNING`, treats a zero-row UPDATE as fatal, allows negative balances, and writes truthful audit + structured evidence in the same transaction. `ExpenseService.approveExpense` rewritten to call V2; client balance read, JS subtraction, `profiles` write, client-built "Balance deducted" audit and status rollback all removed. **V1 deliberately unchanged** — a cached client that still deducts in the browser would otherwise double-deduct. Empty transfer history explicitly NOT treated as evidence (no transfers have been made) and `cash_transfer_history` untouched. 59 new tests. **Rev 2.0 — backend V2 verified LIVE in Production (it was already present; no migration was applied during the server session) and V1 fingerprinted unchanged before/after (SHA-256 `44945d4d…`). MicroLink historical repair COMPLETED 2026-08-08 in one transaction: 6 expenses / ₹880 / 3 employees, balances ₹0 → ₹-180 / ₹-400 / ₹-300, organization total ₹-517 → ₹-1,397 (invariant passed), 6 `historical_repair` deduction rows + 6 corrective audits, original misleading `expense_approved` audits deliberately preserved. Full 14 MB backup verified via `pg_restore --list` (1886 TOC entries); the targeted pre-repair CSVs were empty due to a quoting bug and are recorded as unusable. Rev 3.0 — controlled END-TO-END approval VERIFIED: a real admin approval through the deployed DEV preview hit `POST /rest/v1/rpc/review_approve_expense_v2` (HTTP 200, only `p_expense_id` + `p_comment`) and a read-only PostgreSQL audit confirmed transaction **00088** — ₹34,534.00 deducted, ₹-210,892 → ₹-245,426 matching the live `profiles.balance` and an identical `updated_at`/`created_at` timestamp, **exactly one** deduction row with `source = 'approval'`, truthful audit, correct organization isolation, and no `cash_transfer_history` row. **PRODUCTION-MAIN frontend deployment and Production-domain verification remain PENDING, and runtime concurrency is still unproven** (one approval exercises the locks but cannot show they serialise contention). `get_backend_health` 404 noted as a separate PostgREST routing follow-up.** |

Central `chunkRecovery.ts` adds the Firefox matcher, one shared guarded-reload primitive, and a `vite:preloadError` handler registered early in `main.tsx`; ErrorBoundary self-heals version skew without a noisy monitoring event but still reports a stale error that persists past the guarded reload. Three root causes, all design choices rather than subtle bugs: `captureConsoleIntegration` turned **all 229 `console.error` + 111 `console.warn`** calls into first-class issues; `use-toast` auto-reported **all 229 destructive toasts**; and `sourcemap: false` left every production frame minified. Fixes: console capture removed (console stays as breadcrumbs), toast auto-reporting removed (breadcrumb only — monitoring moves to the catch block that owns the technical context), and the redundant manual registration of `browserApiErrors`/`globalHandlers` dropped after verifying in the installed @sentry/react 10.58.0 source that both are already defaults. Structured `reportError(error, {area, operation, errorCode})` with `normalizeError` (Error/Supabase/Auth/string/unknown, never `[object Object]`), stable fingerprints excluding dynamic IDs so transactions 00088 and 00102 form one issue while different error codes stay apart, ID-only user scope cleared on sign-out, and recursive secret scrubbing. `sourcemap: "hidden"` plus a GlitchTip CLI debug-ID upload script; the build **always** prunes `.map` files from `dist/` — including without credentials — because `hidden` stops the browser requesting them but not their deployment. Non-secret `__APP_RELEASE__` / `__APP_DEPLOY_ENV__` injected so preview and Production are filterable. 92 tests. **Source-map upload NOT live — build-only `SENTRY_URL`/`SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN`/`SENTRY_ORG`/`SENTRY_PROJECT` still required in Vercel; nothing deployed; no existing GlitchTip issue touched.** |

| DOC-095 | Cloud Maintenance Control Plane — architecture + real Cloud verification + admin dashboard V1 + release-blocker fix + maintenance history | [`../docs/maintenance/CLOUD_MAINTENANCE_CONTROL_PLANE.md`](../docs/maintenance/CLOUD_MAINTENANCE_CONTROL_PLANE.md) *(exception: lives in `docs/maintenance/`, not `Documentations MD/`, per explicit owner instruction — a standalone architecture artifact rather than a standard feature doc)* | 2026-08-15 | in progress | `feature/cloud-maintenance-control-plane` only, not merged. A second, fully independent Supabase Cloud project (never PesoWise business data) used solely to answer "is PesoWise in maintenance" — replacing the current Vercel-Edge-Config-coupled system without touching it yet. Phase 1 foundation, real Cloud schema/RLS verification, and dashboard V1 (force/schedule write controls, owner admin authorized, full RLS/audit matrix proven live) landed in earlier revisions. **This revision — RELEASE-BLOCKER FIX (owner-reported): a scheduled ~90-minute window was ending after ~1 minute.** Root cause: every poll unconditionally applied the pure fail-open resolver, so one transient Cloud read failure during an ALREADY-ACTIVE window was indistinguishable from "Cloud says normal" and instantly unmounted the maintenance screen. Reproduced first with a regression test that failed pre-fix. Fixed with a new stateful trusted-lease layer (`maintenanceLeaseState.ts`): a fresh success establishes an in-memory lease bounded by `ends_at` (scheduled) or a 3-minute failure-grace window (forced); only another fresh success or the lease's own bounded expiry can end it — a brand-new tab with no lease still fails open exactly as before. Exact local timers (not polling) now own start/end transition precision; polling reduced to 120s (was 60s/20s) since it no longer needs to. Proven live end-to-end against the real Cloud project: exact-start and exact-end timers fired with zero manual reload, and `window.fetch` was monkey-patched live to simulate the owner's exact transient-failure scenario — the maintenance screen survived two consecutive failures and correctly returned to normal only at the real `ends_at`. **Maintenance history shipped**: new Cloud migration 002 (`maintenance_history` table, `can_view_history` admin permission separate from managing maintenance, atomic `SECURITY DEFINER` trigger-only writes — no application role has any direct write grant), a paginated read service, and a reworked admin console (Control/History tabs, summary cards, a monthly downtime chart via the existing `recharts` dependency, filters, detail drawer) — full lifecycle (create/edit-in-place/cancel/new-after-cancel/force-start/force-end) and RLS matrix proven live inside a rolled-back transaction, byte-identical before/after. Public screen gained an original locally-bundled SVG illustration (reduced-motion aware, zero external assets) and explicit DD/MM/YYYY date formatting throughout. Fixed the uppercase-route hardening gap (`/ADMIN/MAINTENANCE/LOGIN` now correctly routes to the admin app). Production confirmed byte-identical (`updated_at` unchanged) throughout every live test in this revision. Real credentials/UUID never committed anywhere in tracked source. **Blocked on owner action**: no live Cloud Auth sign-in has been performed (only the owner can type that password) — dashboard-while-signed-in QA and auth-isolation-while-signed-in remain outstanding; public self-service signup status not confirmed (no safe CLI read path — manual Dashboard toggle handed to owner). |

Fixed by renaming to a 50-char name and updating the one frontend call site. Also found and closed a security-hardening gap affecting 11 admin RPCs across this whole feature (including two pre-dating this session): a database-level default-privileges rule had silently granted `anon` raw EXECUTE that every migration's `REVOKE FROM PUBLIC` never actually revoked (not an active vulnerability — in-function admin checks already blocked anon — but a real defense-in-depth gap). All five migrations (batch ops, configured-only filter, name fix, overload cleanup, anon revoke) applied and verified live. 227/228 targeted tests pass; full quality gates clean. Pushed to `origin/hotfix/form-template-server-pagination`, not merged to `main`. |
| DOC-101 | Daily Project Report — Server-Side Scalability at 9,000+ Expenses | [`daily-project-report-server-scalability.md`](./daily-project-report-server-scalability.md) | 2026-08-20 | in progress | The Daily Project Report was silently producing incomplete workbooks. Root-caused from Production `pg_stat_statements` (read-only): (1) the expense query in `buildDailyProjectExpenseReport.ts` had no `.range()`/`.limit()`, so PostgREST injected its own `LIMIT` — the deployment cap is 1,000, already proven by HAR in DOC-074 — while MicroLink has **10,111** approved expenses, so at most ~9.9% of the data reached the workbook, with no error shown; (2) the custom-field enrichment loop (75 ids/request, sequential) had run **51,386 calls / 12,267 s cumulative / max 7,984.9 ms** against the `authenticated` role's **8,000 ms** `statement_timeout` — the requests that "stop before the dataset returns". The DB itself was never slow: the same enrichment done set-based takes **349 ms** for the whole org. Implemented: new versioned RPC `get_daily_project_report_page_v1` returning bounded pages inside one jsonb envelope (PostgREST cannot truncate a scalar), resolving tenant scope, report permission (previously browser-only), filters, set-based enrichment and the real `(trackDate, user_id, projectId)` report-row grouping server-side; keyset cursor over a pinned `created_at` snapshot; four helper functions mirroring the client's mapping/bucketing rules; one EXPLAIN-justified partial index. New client export coordinator (`dailyProjectReportPaging.ts`) with single-flight, AbortController cancel, same-cursor retry, progress, duplicate detection and a hard `unique identities === total_count` gate — any gap, duplicate, snapshot change or page failure throws and **no file is produced**. Output parity is structural: the grouping tail was extracted into a shared `finalizeDailyReportGroups` that both pipelines call, with the locale-sensitive sort and `percent` kept in JS. Verified read-only on Production: 10,111 expenses → 4,160 rows, 4,160 distinct keys, amounts identical (₹2,281,670.27), keyset boundary exact. 35 new tests incl. a 9,000-row completeness proof (36 pages, 0 missing, 0 duplicates). **Migration written but NOT applied — and never executed against any database**; no browser QA yet (see §11). Branch `hotfix/daily-project-report-server-scalability`, not merged, not pushed. |

Rebuilt the source's two/three-step "create RPC, then separately save custom fields, then optionally verify/approve" architecture — a documented gap where a failed second call leaves an already-created, already-reviewer-notified expense with a permanently missing field — as a single atomic, idempotent `admin_submit_expense_for_employee_v2` SECURITY DEFINER RPC: authorization, expense creation, custom-field persistence (delegating to the existing `replace_expense_form_field_values` inline, same transaction, not reimplemented) and an optional Submitted/Verified/Approved status transition (delegating to the existing, unmodified `review_admin_verify_expense`/`review_approve_expense_v2` — no financial logic rewritten) all commit or roll back together. New `admin_submit_expense_idempotency` table (not present in the source at all) makes retries safe: a rolled-back attempt reserves no key, a genuine retry after failure is never blocked, a replayed success returns the original result. Actor (admin, `auth.uid()`) and subject (employee, `p_employee_id` → `expenses.user_id`) are never conflated; hierarchy routing is computed from the EMPLOYEE's own reporting/team context, independently re-verified line-by-line against this repo's own `src/lib/approvalHierarchy.ts` before reuse, never the admin's. Ported and fixed both real security defects the source branch found: `audit_logs`' SELECT RLS admin branch used a global `has_role(admin)` check with no organization boundary at all (confirmed this codebase has the identical gap, independently of the source's own claim) — narrowed to an org-scoped check via `organization_memberships`, owner/assigned-engineer branches untouched; `finalize-expense-receipts`'s ownership check was strict-owner-only (`expense.user_id = auth.uid()`, inherited unmodified legacy code), silently rejecting every admin-delegated receipt finalize behind the browser's own designed-in fallback with no visible error — fixed to authorize the owner OR an active admin membership of the expense's organization (source-only, not redeployed). Explicitly NOT ported: the source's live-Excel-dropdown roster generator (`exceljs` dependency) — replaced with a CSV-only template plus a plain reference block, since the dropdown UX has no bearing on server-side correctness and every value is re-validated authoritatively by the RPC regardless of how the file was produced; and the source's narrower receipts Storage INSERT policy fix, because this codebase's CURRENT policy (`bucket_id = 'receipts' AND auth.uid() IS NOT NULL`, no ownership/org scoping at all) is already broader than what the source patched in — porting it would have been a regression, not a fix; documented as a separate, pre-existing, out-of-scope over-permissioning concern rather than silently narrowed. Segregation of duties: an admin who both creates and approves the same expense writes a distinct, machine-searchable `admin_submitted_and_approved_for_employee` audit action (not only a comment string), gated behind a mandatory typed reason in the UI. `>1,000`-row option-branch config fetch reuses a `.range()`-paginated pattern, verified via a mocked client at 0/1/499/500/501/999/1000/1001/1999/2000/2001/5000 rows — exact counts, zero duplicates, correct termination at every boundary. Reused the employee's own field engine (`CategoryFormFieldsSection`, `BaseExpenseFieldInput`, `useOnDemandOptionBranches`) and receipt primitives (`FileUpload`, the fixed `finalize-expense-receipts`) rather than forking either; a "finalized" storage result whose attachment-metadata insert fails is counted as failed, never as silent success. History tab is server-paginated (25/page) with an authoritative `count: "exact"` scoped to the active search. 84 new tests across 5 files: SQL/Edge-Function structural-contract assertions mirroring this codebase's established source-content-assertion pattern, CSV bulk-import parsing/validation including zero-and-multiple-match identity-ambiguity blocking, option-branch pagination boundaries, service-layer error classification (every RPC failure mapped to a specific code, never a generic message)/idempotency/bulk-chunking/receipt-evidence-integrity, and segregation-of-duties dialog enforcement. Zero new TS errors (615 baseline unchanged), zero new lint issues, build clean, two consecutive full-suite runs both 3858 passed/0 failed (up from DOC-108's 3774 baseline by exactly the 84 new tests). Central migration register (`PENDING_MIGRATIONS_PREET_INTEGRATION.md`) updated with 2 new migrations (§5c) and a final Preet branch closure table (§9) explicitly marking `Preet-Patel/feature/balance-ledger-bulk-import` SUPERSEDED / NO INTEGRATION REQUIRED, since the current balance architecture (Historical Balance Anchors V2, Tally Group Summary import) already contains equivalent, more thoroughly hardened functionality. No migration applied, no data touched, no Edge Function deployed. |

Idle CPU on the edge-functions container measured ~0.01%. Stale-job recovery (both the requeue and the exhausted-attempts→failed paths) verified via a `BEGIN...ROLLBACK`-wrapped synthetic test that persisted nothing. No new grants, no service_role exposure in frontend code, no new publicly reachable endpoint. `tsc --noEmit` clean. |

New pure `computeTallyReconciliation(result, employeesById)` resolves Employee ID as always-authoritative identity and returns the importable/skipped split; `reconciles()` is now identity-independent by construction. `ImportBalancesDialog.tsx`'s reconciliation panel shows Tally Grand Total / all-rows / Importable / Skipped separately with "N rows will be skipped. Separately found and fixed a real, independently-confirmed bug in `balanceHistoricalV2Capability.ts`: a `ready: false` result was cached at module scope for the tab's entire lifetime with no distinction from a definitive `ready: true`, so a tab that probed before a backend became ready (or during a transient RPC/network failure) could stay stuck showing "Preview only" forever; fixed with an asymmetric cache (true cached permanently, false cached for only 60s so the same tab self-heals without a reload). 100% read-only against Production (SSH inspection + identity cross-check only, `BEGIN TRANSACTION READ ONLY`); no migration applied, no schema/config/feature-flag mutation, no Edge Function deployed, no container restarted. 79 new/updated tests across 3 files (parser, capability cache, component wizard) plus an exact reproduction of the real file's scenario; the real file itself was run through the fixed parser via `vite-node` confirming `sheetFinancialTotal` now matches `reportGrandTotal` exactly (difference ₹0.00); `tsc --noEmit`/build/`git diff --check`/`migrations:check`/`security:secrets` all clean; full suite passes (3887, one unrelated pre-existing flaky test passes on isolated rerun). Expense deductions now positioned on `expenses.trip_start` (never approval time) with `expenses.transaction_number` exposed; running balance reconstructed server-side over the full chronological event set before pagination (proven identical for page1+page2 vs unpaginated); a reversed credit and its reversal excluded from normal history generically for either engine. Organization-wide reconciliation: 414/415 MicroLink employees reconcile exactly; the one mismatch (employee 1001, "MicroLink Admin", zero ledger/deduction rows but a nonzero balance) is a pre-existing, unrelated data-integrity gap, investigated and explicitly left uncorrected. `ImportBalancesDialog.tsx`/`ImportHistoryDialog.tsx`/`EmployeeBalanceHistory.tsx` rewired to the new engine with additive-framed copy; "Last Credit"/"Recent Credits" cards flagged as a scoped-out follow-up (still read a separate legacy feed). Heavily tested on a throwaway restored-backup database with real `auth.uid()` RPC calls before touching Production (arithmetic, idempotent apply/reversal, admin-only rejection, same-day ordering, pagination consistency, the owner's exact July/August/credit scenario). `tsc`/build/`git diff --check`/`migrations:check`/`security:secrets` clean; full suite 3887 passing (2 unrelated pre-existing flaky tests pass on isolated rerun). No new real credit applied to any employee (per instruction). See §11 of the doc for full detail. |
| DOC-115 | Review Expenses — Manager Approval Split-Brain Fix & Post-Approval UI Reconciliation | [`review-verified-ready-classification.md`](./review-verified-ready-classification.md) | 2026-09-02 | deployed | Renumbered from DOC-113 on merge to `main` (collision with the Tally/Additive-Credit work independently registered as DOC-113/DOC-114). On `hotfix/review-verified-ready-classification`, branched from `origin/main`. Root cause (final, corrected): Bulk Approve's preview RPC (`review_preview_approval_targets_v1` → `_debug_review_actionability_v1`) and the actual approve mutation (`review_approve_expense` → `expense_manager_can_review`) used two independently-evolved readiness rules — the mutation's reporting-chain-aware visibility check recognized a caller as a valid final-tier Manager in cases the preview's literal-assignment-match check did not — so a genuinely approvable expense could show as "waiting for previous verification" in Bulk Approve while the same caller's Approve click would actually succeed. (An earlier attempt on this branch, `20260902070000_review_engineer_tier_assigned_reviewer_bypass.sql`, had misdiagnosed this as a role-vs-assignment mismatch and widened authorization to trust any assigned reviewer regardless of role; proven to have no legitimate use case in real data and fully reverted before the real fix, together with its two frontend mirrors.) Fixed by extracting one shared canonical helper (`_review_manager_normal_approval_ready_v1`, migration `20260902120000_review_canonical_manager_approval_ready.sql`) that both the preview and mutation paths call, so they can never diverge again; no schema/data change, no routing metadata rewritten, no expenses mass-repaired. Verified live against representative Production expenses: preview and mutation now agree. A same-branch follow-up (`fix(review): reconcile completed bulk approvals`) then closed a separate client-lifecycle defect surfaced by owner QA: the Bulk Approve confirmation dialog stayed open after a successful approval (allowing a stale-ID replay, which the server correctly rejected as "already approved" but the client misreported as a failure), and three separate client caches (in-memory review-list cache, its localStorage mirror, and the return-navigation cache) were never invalidated after a mutation, so far pages of a filtered queue could keep showing already-approved rows as "Level 2 Verified." Fixed by closing the dialog before the first network await, treating a fresh "already approved" response as terminal reconciliation rather than failure, evicting settled rows and invalidating all three caches immediately, awaiting the canonical query refresh before releasing the loading state, and clamping a filtered queue to its new last page when approvals shrink it below the currently-viewed page. Read-only Production verification (no expenses mutated by this session): the last 10 real approvals in `audit_logs` all show `status = 'approved'` on the corresponding `expenses` row — the backend was always correct; the entire second defect was frontend cache/pagination state. Full quality gates clean at merge time: `tsc --noEmit` (616 pre-existing baseline errors, no new ones), `vitest run` (3,892/3,892 passing across 222 files), production build, `migrations:check`, `security:secrets`, `git diff --check`. See the doc's §13/§14 (SQL-side and frontend-side corrections) and "Post-approval reconciliation follow-up" section for full detail. |
| DOC-116 | Detailed Expense — Canonical Additive V3 Balance Timeline | [`detailed-expense-canonical-balance.md`](./detailed-expense-canonical-balance.md) | 2026-09-02 | deployed | Renumbered from DOC-115 on merge to `main` (collision — `main` had independently registered DOC-115 for the Review Expenses work). On `hotfix/detailed-expense-canonical-balance`, branched from `main` @ `4c668e4`. Retires the Detailed Expense worker's own second balance-reconstruction algorithm (a backward walk from `rows[0].user_balance` for one employee; a Debit-only flat list with no closing balance at all for "All employees") — both predated Additive Credit V3 and could not see V3 positive/negative balance entries, opening credits, Tally additions, or the V3 reversed-pair visibility rules. New forward-only migration `20260902130000_report_balance_timeline_v3.sql` introduces `_balance_timeline_events_v3`, one canonical event constructor, and rewires both consumers onto it: `_balance_unified_timeline_v3` (Balance Ledger, signature/return/ordering unchanged) and the new service_role-only `report_balance_timeline_rows_v3`. The report builds the complete per-employee timeline, ranks and sums it, and only then applies the date window and paginates, so the first in-period row opens at its true carried-in balance instead of zero; Credit/Debit follow the SIGN of `amount_delta`, never the event type. **Re-verified against current Production rather than trusting the branch's own documentation** (which had recorded the migration apply and worker deploy as blocked/outstanding): both are in fact already live — all four new functions' `pg_get_functiondef` output and the deployed `expenseReports.ts`/`index.ts` are byte-for-byte identical to the branch source (md5 match), grants exactly match the required contract (the three internal functions revoked from `anon`/`authenticated`, granted only to `service_role`; the two public V3 wrappers unchanged), and real `detailed_expense` report jobs had already run to completion on Production before this merge (12,917-row all-employees export, a 387-row single-employee export, an 8,097-row date-windowed export — all `completed`, zero errors), with `expense_balance_deductions` independently confirmed 100% `approved`-status-only (13,254/13,254). **`cash_transfer_history` question, re-audited and root-caused rather than left as a flag:** of the table's 395 rows, only 22 involve an employee as either party (`admin_to_employee`/`cashier_to_employee`/`employee_to_cashier`); the remaining 373 are staff cash-custody handoffs between admin/cashier/engineer roles, unrelated to any individual employee's wallet. Traced the actual write path: the legacy pre-ledger `accept_balance_transfer_offer` RPC performed a raw `UPDATE profiles SET balance = ...` on both parties and logged to `cash_transfer_history`, with **no** `balance_ledger` row — explaining exactly why 0 rows link by `source_id` (there was never a ledger row to link to, not a key-naming mismatch). Because the V3 baseline reconstruction is `profiles.balance − Σ(balance_ledger.amount_delta)`, every such pre-ledger event is mathematically absorbed into each employee's reconstructed carried-in balance by construction — current calculated balances are therefore provably correct; only the itemized historical breakdown omits these events as line items (the newer, ledger-integrated transfer RPCs like `cashier_return_money_to_admin_v2` do write both tables together, and those transfers do appear). Confirmed presentation/history limitation, not an accounting defect; **no backfill performed** — backfilling would itself require adjusting the baseline formula to avoid double-counting money already reflected in `profiles.balance`, and is not needed for balance correctness. Balance Ledger parity proven row-for-row against Production (266 employees, 13,430 rows, 0 differences, 0 positional mismatches) in a rolled-back transaction before the migration was applied; 17-point Production test matrix green including five-employee ledger parity, >1,000-row pagination independence, and the pre-period carried-in-balance proof. 78 automated tests; full quality gates re-verified clean after merging current `main` into the branch. No financial value modified by this branch or by this merge. |
| DOC-117 | Custom Field Submission Data-Integrity Incident (Project Unique ID / Project ID / Project Name) | [`custom-field-submission-data-integrity-incident.md`](./custom-field-submission-data-integrity-incident.md) | 2026-09-05 | deployed (prevention) / historical remediation PENDING | On `hotfix/custom-field-data-integrity`. MicroLink's Project Unique ID → Project ID / Project Name cascading field group intermittently (in the edit/resubmit case, deterministically) lost the dependent values on save, across all four submission paths (single/edit, batch, admin-delegated). Root cause: `useOnDemandOptionBranches` exposed a pending branch-config fetch identically to "no conditional fields" (`optionBranches.length === 0` used everywhere as the signal), so a pending or never-triggered fetch silently computed `visibleBranchIds = new Set()` and dropped both dependents together — explaining the always-both-or-neither pattern. Three distinct client defects confirmed: a race at submit time (Defect B); a *deterministic* one on edit/resubmit, where branch DEFINITIONS were never re-fetched for an already-selected option, only previously-saved VALUES (Defect C — the dominant cause, 100% reproducible, no race needed); and a reconciliation gap where later-arriving branch config never re-derived branch values (Defect D). No request-generation tracking existed at all (Defect E). Separately, and independently, `ExpenseService.submitExpenseBatch` transitioned an expense to `status = 'submitted'` **before** writing its custom field values (two separate round trips, no rollback on a later failure) — the likely mechanism behind the confirmed batch cluster of 4 sibling expenses sharing one correct Project Unique ID but all missing both dependents; the batch shared-field capture mechanism itself (`BatchSharedCategoryFields`/`applyBatchFieldPlanToLines`) was traced in full and found structurally sound. Fixed: `useOnDemandOptionBranches` rewritten with an explicit per-(template,value) status state machine and a generation counter (latest-selection-wins); `CategoryFormFieldsSection`'s reconciliation now gates on every currently-selected template being confirmed `ready`, never inferred from an empty array; `ExpenseForm.tsx`'s edit bootstrap reordered to load existing values before prefetching branch definitions (reusing its own existing `preserveFormValues` mechanism); `saveFormFieldValues` and pre-submit validation now block outright on any unresolved selection; `AdminSubmitExpense.tsx` (previously with zero required-field validation and a second, divergent, unfiltered config source) now shares the same on-demand config + validation; `submitExpenseBatch`'s write order fixed (values before status transition). New canonical server-side guarantee — the permanent, browser-independent backstop the incident spec required as "the most important part" — added via `20260905150000_validate_expense_custom_fields_on_submit.sql` (**not applied to Production**): one validator function reproducing the app's own proven `assignment.required ?? template.required` / `show_on_submit` semantics (traced from code + existing tests CC-5/CC-6, never guessed) and resolving the exact active branch chain for whatever value is saved, wired via two `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` constraint triggers on `expenses` (split INSERT/UPDATE — Postgres disallows `OLD` in a combined trigger's WHEN clause) firing only on a transition into `submitted`; deferred specifically because `admin_submit_expense_for_employee_v2` writes the expense row before its custom fields within one transaction. 10 new/updated tests passing (hook state-machine incl. latest-selection-wins, `CategoryFormFieldsSection` reconciliation, a characterization test proving the exact drop mechanism); zero regressions across 9 existing suites; `tsc` baseline unchanged (616); full end-to-end `ExpenseForm` edit-mode integration test explicitly attempted and deferred (not faked) as a documented gap. September 2026 MicroLink dry-run (read-only, zero writes): 1,164 complete / 0 partial / 28 both-dependents-missing (all 28 confirmed genuinely required, not optional) / 6 UID-itself-absent (not investigated, no signal to infer from). The 28 subdivided: 18 `DETERMINISTIC_REPAIR_CANDIDATE` (exactly one historical (Project ID, Project Name) pair for that UID value org-wide), 10 `AMBIGUOUS_MULTIPLE_PAIRS_DO_NOT_GUESS` (2 distinct historical pairs exist), 0 unrecoverable. No repair SQL written or executed, no Production mutation of any kind, `main` not merged, awaiting owner authorization before any migration apply or repair. **CLOSURE 2026-09-07: prevention DEPLOYED — 20260905150000 live in Production, client fixes merged in main 18bd424, canonical validator live and now shared with DOC-119's post-submission guard. HISTORICAL REMEDIATION REMAINS PENDING; no row was repaired.** |
| DOC-119 | Organization-Level Mandatory Expense Fields | [`organization-mandatory-expense-fields.md`](./organization-mandatory-expense-fields.md) | 2026-09-07 | deployed | On `hotfix/org-required-expense-fields`, worktree branched from `origin/main`. Generic, organization-scoped policy letting a tenant declare a form field mandatory on EVERY expense — every category, including categories created later — enforced server-side on any transition into `submitted`, from any client, RPC or direct SQL. Exists because DOC-117's validator is structurally per-category: "required everywhere" can only be written as N assignment rows kept in sync by hand, and it resolves `expenses.category` (text) to a category row BY NAME, so a row whose category string matches nothing resolves to zero requirements and passes unchecked (Production carries two such orphan strings from renames). New `organization_required_expense_fields` table keyed on stable identifiers only — template UUID for the parent (templates carry an empty field_key here) and branch `field_key` for the dependents (stable across 529/525 per-option rows) — never mutable display names, and no organization name anywhere in the mechanism. Reuses DOC-117's single deferred trigger entry point rather than adding a competing one; runs policy-first so the tenant sees a deterministic PW001 with a user-safe message naming the missing fields, not a generic 23514. Read-only pre-enablement audit passed: all 10 active categories expose the parent field and all 237 project options used in 60 days expose both dependents. 17/17 acceptance cases on a restored Production database with deferred triggers forced to fire (including incomplete-draft-allowed, admin-delegated atomic failure, batch, approved-historical-untouched, orphan-category bypass closed, and a control tenant proven unaffected); historical fingerprint unchanged. | DOC-125 | Atomic Expense Persistence V2 — MicroLink Single-Expense V1 | [`atomic-expense-persistence-v2.md`](./atomic-expense-persistence-v2.md) | 2026-09-09 | deployed | **MERGED TO MAIN (`bf7be6c`) AND LIVE ON PRODUCTION for MicroLink single-expense submission** — new expense, existing-draft submit, edit/resubmit, desktop and mobile. MicroLink batch client remains legacy (its V1 server endpoint exists and is tested; the client cutover is the explicit next task). Every other organization (Mistry And Shah, Patidar Travels, Bikes auto Hero, Trial) is unchanged, routed by one function resolved on organization id. A real `ReferenceError` the owner found on the first Preview click (`requiresAttachment` read outside its defining function's scope) was fixed and verified present before merge. Pre-merge: 0 drift from `origin/main`, tsc clean, build passes, 43/43 focused tests, 4051/4052 full suite (1 independently proven pre-existing flake — byte-identical file, passes in isolation), 0 new lint debt. Confirmed via the live Production JS bundle itself, not a SHA label, that the V1 RPC names are actually deployed. No MicroLink QA employee exists, so no real-employee smoke test was run; the one existing receipt from the owner's own Preview QA was instead verified in full: 5 fields persisted (Project Unique ID, server-derived Project ID/Name, From, To), 1 audit event, 0 canonical-validator issues. Legacy path fully retained for rollback. Originally on `architecture/atomic-expense-persistence-v2`, branched from `origin/main` @ `90b7723`. The mandated inventory that must precede any implementation. An expense is one business object written through **three separate client-orchestrated network mutations** — INSERT/UPDATE `expenses`, then `replace_expense_form_field_values`, then a `PATCH` setting `status='submitted'`. When the submission guard refuses the third, the first two have already committed and the row stays a draft: exactly the 2026-09-08 employee-6818 incident (six refused submissions, Rs 2,590 stranded). Decisive finding: **no server function sets `status='submitted'` for an ordinary employee** — that transition exists only as a client PATCH, so there is no atomic command to harden and one must be built. Core columns survived historically because they are written by the same statement that creates the row; custom fields did not, because they live in another table, are written by another call, and their shape depends on asynchronously fetched template and branch configuration. Mapped all 9 client write paths and all 16 server functions that write `expenses` or `expense_form_field_values`. The schema already supplies most of what V2 needs: UNIQUE (expense_id, template_id) and (expense_id, branch_field_id) make narrow ON CONFLICT upserts possible, `assign_transaction_number` fires inside whichever transaction performs the transition, the DEFERRABLE constraint triggers already validate rows actually written at COMMIT, and `admin_submit_expense_idempotency` is a working pattern to generalise; missing are a revision column and a submission receipt table. Two items documented rather than assumed: batch partial-success is existing product behaviour (an explicit stop — owner decision required before touching batch semantics), and `anon` holds EXECUTE on four mutating functions (separate hotfix). No code changed, no migration written, 0 business rows touched. |

Fixes a real format-inconsistency bug: `buildDetailedCsv` used to drop `Employee`/`Employee ID` columns whenever a single employee was selected, silently making "All employees" and "Employee = X" two different report formats — now `Employee`/`Employee ID` are always present, selecting an employee only ever filters the DATASET. Adds an on-page paginated preview (50 rows/page) that auto-loads the moment generation completes, reading a new `report_export_job_rows` table the worker populates from the SAME row objects it builds the CSV from (never a live re-query) — deliberately, because `report_balance_timeline_rows_v3` computes Opening/Closing Balance backwards from the LIVE wallet balance with no snapshot-boundary parameter, so a later re-query with identical filters could silently disagree with the already-downloaded file. New RPCs: `store_report_export_job_preview_chunk_v1`/`clear_report_export_job_preview_v1` (worker-only), `get_report_export_job_preview_v1` (authenticated, same authorization rule as the existing history RPC, resolved from the job's own stored organization_id). Report History moves from a flat top-20/100 fetch to a real 5-per-page pager via new `list_report_export_jobs_v2` (adds offset + a real total_count; `list_report_export_jobs_v1` is untouched). Preview is Detailed-Expense-only for this release; the other three report types simply show no preview section. Migration logic proven via a rolled-back transaction against the shared database (chunk-boundary math with no overlap/gaps, anon rejected at the GRANT layer, cross-org rejected, not-completed graceful degrade, history-v2 total_count) — zero permanent footprint, **not yet permanently applied to any environment**. 211 tests across every report-related test file pass; full suite 4328/4328 (zero failures); tsc/build/migrations:check/security:secrets clean. Built on branch `feature/recent-expense-field-values` (reused, per explicit instruction, for one integrated Preview deployment alongside DOC-130 Expense Field Memory) — **not merged to `main`.** |

| 2026-08-06 | DOC-086 Phase A — `convert-heic` rewritten on `dev` to remove in-isolate HEIC decoding; no live change. The native path was probed first, as instructed, and imgproxy answered "Source image resolution is too big" — not an unsupported format, proving libheif was present all along and the architecture was valid. The controlled receipt measures 3768x5800 = 21.9 MP, i.e. 83 MB of RGBA against a 150 MB worker cap, so the earlier copy-elimination could never have sufficed: the full-resolution decode inside a sandboxed JS heap was itself the defect. Removed `heic-decode`, `jpeg-js`, RGBA materialisation, pixel-buffer handling and `jpeg.encode`; the function now authenticates, validates the path, probes the cache, sniffs 16 header bytes, calls the Storage render endpoint (2000x2000, contain, quality 82, `Accept: image/jpeg,image/webp`, never `format=origin` since origin is the unrenderable format) and stores whichever format actually came back — `.jpg`/`image/jpeg` or `.webp`/`image/webp`. `IMGPROXY_AUTO_WEBP=true` here, so the format is negotiated and must never be assumed; WebP is never stored under a false JPEG content type. Cache and client both probe `.jpg` before `.webp` so pre-WebP renditions stay valid. Typed `TRANSFORM_FAILED` (422) replaces the opaque 500; logging carries only request id, stage, timings, byte counts, status and error code, with the storage path reduced to a truncated one-way digest because the path identifies an expense and its owner. The old pixel-buffer tests were replaced, not deleted, by stronger architectural guards asserting the dangerous allocation cannot occur at all. 2356 tests / 117 files; tsc, typecheck:api, build, migrations:check and security:secrets clean; lint 458 baseline unchanged. | 2026-08-06 | DOC-085 rev 5.0 — fixed the HTTP 500 that `convert-heic` returned for every real conversion. Diagnosed from the status code without server access: a missing env var 500s *before* auth and the earlier unauthenticated smoke test had returned 401, a decode failure returns 422 and a missing object 404 — leaving only the encode step, which had no error handling of its own. `jpeg.encode` was being fed `new Uint8Array(decoded.data.buffer)`, and because `heic-decode` is libheif-wasm backed that expression returns the ENTIRE wasm heap rather than the frame, ignoring `byteOffset`/`byteLength`; jpeg-js validates `length === width*height*4`, threw, and the throw escaped into the outer handler as an opaque 500. Replaced with `Uint8Array.from(decoded.data)` — an exact-length copy that also survives wasm memory growth detaching the view — plus an explicit pixel-count check and a dedicated try/catch returning a typed `ENCODE_FAILED` (422) so nothing reaches the outer handler as a 500 again. 7 new tests including a reproduction against a wasm-shaped view and a source guard that strips comments first (the fix's own explanation quotes the dangerous expression and would otherwise satisfy its own guard). 2328 total / 117 files; tsc, eslint, security:secrets clean. No SQL, no migration. **Not deployed — this machine has no SSH access to the Coolify host (the `pesowise_srv` key is rejected); the owner or the VPN-connected machine must deploy.** Next ID: DOC-086. | Agent |

| 2026-08-05 | DOC-085 rev 3.0 — server-side HEIC decode via a new `convert-heic` Supabase edge function, resolving rev 2.0's open decision WITHOUT weakening CSP. CSP is a browser-only policy, so decoding in Deno sidesteps the `new Function` block that makes client-side `heic2any` unusable in production. The function mirrors `compress-upload`'s gate (bearer JWT → getUser → active organization membership) and writes a JPEG to `derived/<path>.jpg`; the client probes that path with a plain public GET and only invokes the function on a miss, so each receipt is decoded ONCE rather than once per view and the historical backlog self-heals as receipts get opened. Deliberately not a general image converter: it accepts an object key inside the receipts bucket only — never a URL, with `://`, absolute paths, `.`/`..` segments and the `derived/` prefix all rejected — and requires the BYTES to sniff as HEIC via `ftyp` brand, because the recorded content_type for a .heic upload is routinely empty or application/octet-stream. Guards: 50 MB input cap, 40 MP output cap (RGBA is 4 bytes/pixel, so 40 MP is ~160 MB before the encoder allocates — refusing beats an OOM-kill surfacing as an opaque 502), `upsert` so a partial rendition self-heals, one-year cacheControl. Client order is native decode → existing rendition → function → in-browser heic2any → unsupported, with every step bounded so an undeployed or unreachable function degrades to the format message rather than hanging. The rendition keys off the path the bytes were ACTUALLY found at, since `candidateReceiptPaths` recovers from stale temp/expense paths and filing against the stored path would point at nothing. Registered in `supabase/config.toml` (verify_jwt=false, handler-side auth) and picked up automatically by `scripts/deploy-edge-functions-coolify.ps1`, which enumerates directories. 4 new tests, 2284 total / 115 files; tsc, eslint and build clean. **Rev 4.0 — DEPLOYED** to the self-hosted Supabase on Coolify (edge-runtime v1.71.2). An unauthenticated POST returned the handler's own `{"errorCode":"UNAUTHORIZED"}` JSON, proving entrypoint, module graph and `_shared/cors.ts` all resolved, and **the esm.sh imports worked** — the `npm:` fallback was not needed. Deploy went in via `docker cp` because the SSH user cannot write `/data/coolify/...` without sudo, so the scp-based `npm run deploy:edge-functions` will hit the same wall under that account. **The decode and upload path has still never executed** — no authenticated conversion, no `public_url` render check, no decode-CPU measurement against the runtime limits (the remaining plausible failure, since `finalize-expense-receipts` already trips `early termination` there). Until then the client degrades to the format message with Open in new tab. Next ID: DOC-086. | Agent |
| 2026-08-05 | DOC-085 rev 2.0 — hotfix for a hang that rev 1.0 shipped to Production. The preview sat on "Loading image…" forever. Cause was not the file: `heic2any` decodes inside a blob Worker whose libheif glue calls `new Function`, and the enforced CSP (verified live on the production domain) is `script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' https://challenges.cloudflare.com` with no 'unsafe-eval'. The worker threw EvalError, died without posting a result, and heic2any's promise never settled — neither resolving nor rejecting — so the awaited resolver never returned and the dialog's `.finally` never cleared the spinner. A try/catch is structurally unable to catch that. Fixed with `settleWithin()` (decode bounded at 20 s, dialog always reaches a terminal state), `isDynamicCodeAllowed()` (probe `new Function` up front and skip both the decoder and its ~1.35 MB chunk when eval is barred, rather than waiting out the timeout on every preview) and `browserDecodesNatively()` (`createImageBitmap` first, so Safari and iOS render the original with no conversion and no CSP involvement). The probe is deliberately not memoised — hidden order-dependent state for microseconds of saving. 3 new tests including one that reproduces the never-settling promise under fake timers; 2280 total / 115 files; tsc, eslint and build clean. **Under the current CSP, Chromium and Firefox now fail fast and honestly (format message + Open in new tab + Download) but still do not RENDER HEIC.** Making them render needs a decision not taken here: 'unsafe-eval' app-wide (weakens CSP for the whole application weeks after the DOC-082/083 remediation) or server-side decode (new endpoint + native dependency). Note the same CSP blocks the upload-time conversion in `uploadCompression.ts`, which likely explains why raw HEIC exists in storage at all. Next ID: DOC-086. | Agent |
| 2026-08-05 | Registered DOC-085 (HEIC / HEIF receipt preview). Reported from Production: opening a HEIC receipt showed "This file is no longer available in storage — the attachment record exists but the file was not recovered during migration", for a file that was in storage and intact. Root cause was not storage: `AttachmentPreviewDialog` carried one `previewError` boolean that both "the blob resolver returned nothing" and "the `<img>` failed to decode these bytes" set, and HEIC — the iPhone camera default — cannot be decoded in an `<img>` by any Chromium or Firefox build. The app was therefore reporting data loss that had not occurred, on the one attachment type most likely to be a phone-photographed receipt. Fixed at read time rather than by a backfill: `resolveImagePreviewSource()` decodes HEIC→JPEG through the `heic2any` dependency upload compression already carries (dynamic import, so it stays a lazy ~341 kB gzip chunk fetched only on the first HEIC preview), detecting by content type, extension, **or** an ISO-BMFF `ftyp` brand sniff of the first 16 bytes — the sniff mattering because storage routinely serves these as `application/octet-stream` with no usable extension. The resolver now returns `ok` / `missing` / `unsupported` plus both a renderable URL and the untouched original, so the `<img>` shows the converted JPEG while Download and Open in new tab always hand back the real uploaded file; `<img>` `onError` sets `unsupported`, never `missing`; and **Open in new tab is no longer PDF-only** — it renders for every resolved attachment and again inside the failure block, which is the requested escape hatch. `resolveImagePreviewObjectUrl` retained as a wrapper (so `AttachmentThumbnail` gains conversion for free) and revokes the orphaned original rather than leaking one object URL per failed preview. A `FileReader` fallback backs `Blob.arrayBuffer()` because jsdom lacks it and the sniff would otherwise pass tests while silently answering "not HEIC". 18 new tests, 2277 total / 115 files (was 2259 / 113); tsc, eslint on changed files and build all clean. No migration, no schema change, no data touched, nothing written back to storage. **Not verified in a browser against a real HEIC receipt — that needs an authenticated session and a HEIC attachment, and the decode path is exercised against a mocked `heic2any` only. Thumbnail lists of many HEIC files will each decode on the main thread; not addressed.** Next ID: DOC-086. | Agent |

(1) `myExpensesPageSchema.ts` silently normalized malformed values (numeric fields fell back to 0 via `Number.isFinite(n) ? n : 0`, several string/boolean fields via `.catch()`) instead of failing — rewrote the numeric schema to reject non-finite values via `z.union([number,string]).transform(...).pipe(z.number().finite())`, and modeled every field's required/nullable-ness from the RPC's actual `jsonb_build_object(...)` shape instead of defensively catching everything; a malformed response now always throws `MyExpensesPageValidationError`. (3) `reviewAppliedQuery.ts`'s month/3m/6m/year/1y relative-time bounds used raw `date.setUTCMonth()`/`setUTCFullYear()`, which silently overflows at month ends (March 31 minus one month became March 3, not February 28, since JS Date setters roll excess days into the next month instead of clamping) — added `subtractUtcMonthsClamped()` which clamps to the target month's actual last day while preserving time-of-day; 7d/30d were already correct (exact day-count subtraction). Moved `ExpenseDateLockSection.tsx`'s hardcoded English into the existing `settings` i18n namespace (en/gu/hi), matching `TeamManagementSection`/`DailyExpenseReportMappingSection`'s own convention. Added 19 new automated contract tests (`src/lib/expenseDateLockTriggerLogic.ts` + `src/lib/__tests__/expenseDateLocking.test.ts`) proving the full effective decision for every reviewed scenario (feature OFF, submitted-inside-range blocked, draft-before-lock-then-submitted blocked, non-submitted INSERT not blocked, unrelated review transitions unaffected, inactive lock, range boundaries, multiple ranges) via a hand-maintained pure mirror of the trigger condition -- this repo has no sandboxed Postgres to run a real SQL integration test, so cross-org isolation and the SQL-side feature-flag gate are explicitly documented as still requiring live/staging database verification rather than silently treated as proven. Tally (DOC-105) was NOT touched this pass -- re-ran its existing 76-test suite unmodified, all passing, confirming the Debit-only semantics are unchanged. `npx tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit`: 620 errors, matching `main`'s baseline exactly, 0 new. `npx vitest run`: 4640/4641 (1 pre-existing, confirmed-unrelated flake). Build/migrations/secrets all clean. Commit `ade11d7`, pushed, PR #7 updated, not merged. | Claude Code |
